At the end of the day, this is, on a second level, a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the law on value added tax, a reduced procedure. ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u2	Mr. Vice-President of the National Assembly, thank you for your floor. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! At its 15th meeting, on 6 November this year, the Finance Committee considered the draft Law amending and supplementing the Value Added Tax Act submitted to the National Assembly for its consideration and adoption by the Group of Members and Members with the first signed by Branet Golubović. In a complementary explanation, the drafter presented the objective and purpose of the proposal, which provides for a reduction in the taxation of books and other publications from the current 9.5 to 5%, and stressed that the proposal was signed by all 90 Members. He further presented substantive solutions in the proposed amendments to the Committee, which received broad political support. With regard to the proposal for a law, he considered that the reduced value added tax rate for books and publications would make it easier for those working in this business area. The representative of the Legislative and Legal Service said that in her opinion she had pointed out the issue of too frequent changes, including newly introduced regulatory solutions, as this had an impact on legal certainty. With regard to the supply of goods, she drew attention to a directive of the Council of the European Union which concluded that the supply of digital publications on physical media and electronic delivery were comparable. The State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance explained the opinion of the Government, which does not object to the proposal of the law, drawing attention to the public financial aspect of the proposed tax reduction, since VAT is the largest tax source of the financing of the budget. The representative of the Commission of the State Council for the Economy, Crafts, Tourism and Finance has taken up a written report in which the Commission supports the proposal of the law. The debate reflected the support of members and members of the committee for the draft law, as the reduction in the tax rate from 9.5% to 5% is a step in the right direction. The importance of the whole treatment of culture, the strengthening of Slovenian language and the importance of reading culture and thus the development of reading skills was highlighted. Some felt that the long-term objective should be a zero rate of tax for books, as it once was. Further questions were raised regarding the possibility of abuse in the definition of supplies of goods and the distinction between advertising content and professional content. Some members questioned whether the reduced tax rate would also lead to a reduction in retail prices of books, and highlighted the issue of reducing tax rates for some other goods, including drinking water. The question was raised which Member States of the European Union have three different tax rates. During and after the discussion, the Secretary of State replied to the questions raised and provided additional clarifications. In accordance with the second paragraph of Article 130 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee considered and adopted the amendments tabled by the LMŠ, SDS, SMC, Desus, SAB, SNS and the Italian and Hungarian National Communities' Emissary Group by Articles 4, 5 and 6. In accordance with Article 131, paragraph 8, of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee considered and adopted the following proposals for amendments to the Committee, namely Article 2 and Article 4 of the Annex. In the light of the amendments adopted, the text of the amended legislation, which includes the amendments adopted, has been prepared pursuant to Article 133(1) of the Rules of Procedure of the General Assembly. The amended bill is an integral part of this report. In accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, the Committee voted on all the articles of the bill and adopted them. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u3	Thank you very much. For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, State Secretary of the Ministry of Finance, Natalija Kovač Jereb. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u4	Dear Vice President, honourable Members, honourable Members! Members with the first signed Branet Golubović submitted to the National Assembly a proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Value Added Tax Act. The aim of the proposal is to introduce a second additional lower value added tax rate of 5% for the supply of printed books, newspapers and periodicals, of which 9.5% of the value added tax is paid after the current application. The proposed reduction in value added tax according to the applicant is a step towards resolving the situation in which newspapers, books and publications were printed. The government does not oppose the proposal, but in its opinion on the proposal for a law, the public financial aspect of the implementation of an additional lower value added tax rate is highlighted in particular. While we can agree that the public financial situation is currently favourable, we must not forget the fundamental principle of responsible public financial policy. Public financial policy is based on respect for the fiscal rule, while it is important that, at the time of economic growth, it is not encouraged by further reducing tax burdens, but by maintaining the general level of burden, by ensuring that savings are created, which will enable all public policies to be implemented in times of crisis, without unnecessarily reducing the scope of rights. It should also be borne in mind that the introduction of an additional lower rate of 5% for the proposed supply of publications on physical media will result in a loss of revenue from value added tax of EUR 8 million at annual level. The application of an additional lower rate of 5% also for the supply of electronic publications, which is announced by the adoption of an amendment already announced by the Finance Committee, will amount to an additional EUR 400,000 of lower value added tax revenue. As the Government has repeatedly pointed out, the debate on amending the list of supplies which are taxed with a lower value added tax in Slovenia, or on the number of lower rates, should be more appropriate following the adoption of the reform of the updated value added tax system by establishing a final value added tax system for cross-border trade within the European Union, in which the reform of the system of value added tax rates is also planned. Ladies and gentlemen, in view of the reservations already raised, the Government has already said that it will not oppose the bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u14	Your Honor! The bill introduces a new, special, lower, 5% VAT rate for the supply of books, newspapers and periodicals, including library rental, on all physical and electronic media in the system of value added taxing. The inclusion of a lower rate for electronic carriers was also accepted on the home working body, on the basis of the addition of the New Slovenia Group. In the Group of the Parties to the Modern Centre, we supported it because we wanted to ensure that this measure, which does not have ideological content, would not be opposed by anyone and would be unanimously adopted. Since the Coalition Treaty sets out a commitment to improve the position of Slovenian publishers and authors, the Group of the Modern Centre Party is a co-proposer in the treatment of this law. The European Union Directive allows the country to have a maximum of two reduced VAT rates and that the minimum may not, as a rule, be less than 5%. By comparison, the vast majority of the Member States of the European Union use a lower VAT rate or even zero VAT rate; 18 Member States use two lower VAT rates, most of which use a second lower rate for books and printed media. Slovenia cannot have a super-reduced or zero rate unless, within the framework of the proposals for a broader VAT reform, the European Union decides that individual Member States will have more opportunities to set VAT rates. At the Modern Centre Party, we expect the Government to defend this position also at Union level. All current ministers of culture have advocated a zero VAT rate for books, which would mean greater accessibility for readers, greater reading promotion and long-term growth of reading culture. The analysis shows that reducing tax burden is one of the most neutral and applied measures by which Member States of the European Union help bookkeeping and the newspaper industry within the European Union. This will facilitate the publication of daily, informational, human, educational and cultural content. The VAT reduction is mainly due to the difficult situation in the print media, especially the daily newspapers and weeks which are faced with less advertising interest and the decline in the load. In this case, we estimate that a tax reduction measure would not represent a privilege in relation to the others; it is a concrete and necessary measure of State support to improve the situation in this area. Of course, many other measures are needed to improve the Slovenian media landscape and strengthen the Slovenian book. Now we'd be doing a great deal of damage if we didn't take advantage of the opportunities we have right now. When weighing the costs or the loss of tax revenue and benefits, the scale is certainly geared towards the direction of benefit. Therefore, the country should be at least as generous as the European Directive allows it. We believe in the Modern Centre Party that this measure should be seen more broadly, especially in terms of strengthening the reading culture and preserving the Slovenian language; and not only from the point of view of the loss of budget revenues. The fact is that the written word represents the backbone of culture and public debate, so it is not only a measure to help bookkeeping and the media industry, but much more. This improves the status of the public word, language development, culture of dialogue, critical thinking, public debate, control of power and so on. Books, newspapers, publications spread our horizons, strengthen our spirit, our intellect, enable our development. Therefore, in the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre, the question of whether reading is worth reading is worth reading is taken as rhetorical and we answer it positively, decisively, assembling. In the Modern Centre Party, we also hope that the law will improve the legal and social situation of journalists. Therefore, we regret that some print media or their publishers, who have announced that journalists have been forgiven or have already begun to do so, have not waited for the law to be passed and to take effect. According to our assessment, the financial effects of the law in their favour should be used to improve the position of journalists or to invest in better quality journalism. On the basis of these arguments, of course, we will be happy to support the law in the Group of the Modern Centre Parties.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u16	Thank you for your word, chairman. Colleagues and colleagues! In Levica we support the introduction of a specific rate of VAT on books, newspapers and periodicals and their electronic versions. This is an urgent crisis measure, with which we can at least temporarily put out the fire that ravages the book and publishing market in Slovenia. Publishing and bookkeeping face drastic changes in reading habits, lowering standards and weakening the industry. Since the crisis, the turnover of the publishing industry has halved and the number of books issued has decreased by one fifth, while the public book agency's funds have decreased by 30%, and funds for the purchase of new materials in libraries have also decreased. Measures to improve the situation of written words are therefore necessary. We have therefore proposed conclusions on the Committee on Culture in Levica on 10 May this year, which we have also agreed with the Ministry of Culture. We have proposed the introduction of an additional reduced VAT rate of 5%, we have also requested more budgetary resources for the operation of the Public Agency for the Book and Activities of Public Libraries, the preparation of measures to improve the accessibility of books and the promotion of reading and additional activities at European Union level to enable the implementation of a zero VAT rate per book. The lowering of the rate is also foreseen for the text of the coalition agreement, where a zero VAT measure for the book is recorded. The book and newspaper market suffered additional losses in the crisis, and many Slovenian newspapers and magazines are on the verge of financial collapse. Between 2012 and 2016, newspaper sales decreased by 27 % and publishing activity dropped by half after the crisis, from 120 million to only 60 million. If we want to keep the book and the press, which represent the intellectual backbone of society, the country will have to support these activities. The book has always represented a city of social criticism, but books published in Slovenia are less and more difficult to publish and more difficult to read. From 2009 to 2017, the number of books published in Slovenia decreased by a fifth, and sales also declined. In parallel, reading achievements of the population and population have declined, as shown by the 2016 PIAAC survey, which found that a quarter of adult Slovenians have difficulty understanding even simple texts, which should be of great concern to us. The state's response has so far been inappropriate. Savings measures also hit the book after the crisis, and the Public Book Agency has as much as 30% less resources than in 2010. The decrease in the public agency's budget for the book from a good 7 to a bad 5 million euro in 2012 under the austerity measures up to and including 2019 has not yet been replaced. The State must start investing again in the book and take measures to enable the publishing industry and the authors to publish books and increase accessibility and sales. The introduction of a reduced VAT rate is one step towards this objective. However, compared to the military, which will receive more than 100 million budget in the next two years, or with private companies and private individuals, which will receive almost half a billion euros in subsidies next year, cultural activity will continue to be financially underfed. In the budgets for 2020 and 2021, Sharčev's government has taken very good care of the arms dealers whose business will continue to flourish and for private capital, which will take advantage of citizens' free gifts; and for cultural people much less. Also, the Sharčev government, with a recent income reform, has donated more than €130 million to the top wage recipients in the country; it gives only €8 million by reducing VAT for the book. Unfortunately, these figures still reflect the wrong budgetary and tax priorities of the current government, which will have to be radically changed in the future. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u18	Thank you very much, Mr. Vice President. Dear representatives of the government, dear colleagues and colleagues! The emissary law, which, of course, was also signed by Members of New Slovenia, originally envisioned a reduction in the VAT rate for books, newspapers, publications on physical media or more precisely – on paper. New Slovenia has continued here, we have adopted or should apply in our lives, the economy – yes, culture – a digital agenda years ago within the framework of the European Union. Of course, we did not miss an agreement last October on the Ecofin Economic and Financial Affairs Council, where our Minister of Finance also sits. And on this Ecofin last October, an agreement was adopted allowing Member States to apply lower, very low or even zero VAT rates to electronic publications and thereby harmonising VAT rules for electronic and physical publications. We in New Slovenia understood this as an important progress in the efforts to modernise the VAT system within the European Union for the digital age, if you want. Under the current VAT rules, electronic services are taxed at a general VAT rate of at least 15%, while non-standard rates may apply to publications on a physical medium. The government had already proposed to the National Assembly a new Law on Value Added Tax, which somehow forgot that we are creating a single market, a single digital market in Europe. And this agreement I mentioned at Ecofin last October then gave birth to a European directive, which brings about the following solutions. For physical publications, books, newspapers and magazines, Member States have the option to apply a lower VAT rate, which is at least 5%. Some were allowed to apply very low VAT rates, which is below 5%, or zero rates, which include VAT deductions. Interestingly, this Directive was on its first birthday on the day of the Committee on Finance's meeting, when we considered this amendment of the Value Added Tax Act, this Directive will also allow Member States wishing to do so to use lower VAT rates for electronic publications. Very low and zero rates will only be allowed to the Member States currently using them for physical publications. In short, Nova Slovenija has helped us to upgrade the law, so we now have a 5% VAT rate not only for publications on physical media, on paper, but also for publications in electronic form. It was no problem for us to withdraw our amendment on the committee, withdraw it, under prior agreement, that our amendment should be supported by all the parliamentary groups. And that's how it happened, and then all the members of the group tabled this amendment, which follows the digital economy, the digital society. This is one example, once again, of how we can do something good if we walk together. Even if for the time being the position is still such that the amendment of the Group of Members is not good; well, if we all sign it, then it is good. It is therefore perfectly normal for us to support the amendment of the Law on Value Added Tax of the Member and Members of the New Slovenia. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u20	Dear President, fellow Members, distinguished Members! With the proposal for a law amending and supplementing the Value Added Tax Act, we are transferring the latest changes in European legislation in the tax field to the national legal order. Thus, the bill for all books, newspapers and publications, regardless of the type of carrier, reduces the VAT rate by 9.5% to a lower rate, which is a 5% rate. This means that this transaction will apply to all books, newspapers, publications on physical and e-carriers. That electronic and printed books, newspapers, publications are subject to the same tax rate, the financial ministers of the European Community decided last October after several years of unsuccessful attempts. In agreement with the bill before us, we also followed it in Slovenia. In fact, it is not logical that the book we are reading on an electronic screen would otherwise be taxed on paper from its printed version. Members of the European Union may apply reduced rates to electronic and printed publications and some countries also know super-reduced and zero VAT rates. In Croatia, Cyprus, Hungary, Malta, Italy, Lithuania and so on, they are already using a 5% tax rate. A lower, 5% tax rate for books, newspapers and publications in a physical or e-form will have far-reaching positive consequences for Slovenian word and reading culture. It is known that in Slovenia we have made little effort in recent years to make books more accessible to the general public. Also, the proportion of people who consume more demanding content and literature is relatively small; but we must be aware that reading books is the basis of an educational, curious, and empathetic person. It is we who must decide what kind of individuals and society we want to become. If the average household spent € 146 in 2008 on the purchase of newspapers and other periodicals, there were only 96 in 2015. The numbers are worrisome. In the last decade, press offices have also been heavily decimated. Many printed media have been in trouble; on the other hand, we have witnessed a flood of information and a team of misleading, often free internet content. Nor does the measure go on as a privilege, but as an urgent and concrete support of the country to the media, journalism, publishers, writers and other content creators. It is about promoting creativity of content, improving the position of authors and publishers and strengthening Slovenian language and reading culture in the broadest sense of the word. And for the preservation of critical and intellectual readers: for he that readeth, these thoughts he that readth understandeth better himself, and other men, and the world in which he dwelleth. Reading contributes significantly to the development of all other language abilities and to the personal development of an individual, so it is very important that children, as early as childhood, develop an interest in reading, approach the book and encourage them to read. The Slovenian language has been preserved in history as a distinct language and has grown from the language of farmers to the principal foundation of the nation. It is part of a national identity that we must be proud of and preserve at all costs. We believe that this introduction of a lower tax rate for the supply of books, newspapers and periodicals on the physical and e-carriers of Slovenia will give a new impetus, so in the Parliamentary Group of Alenka Bratušek, we unanimously support the proposal of the law. Finally, let me add that, under the proposal for a law, we have signed all the members of the National Assembly, which I consider to be very positive and state-of-the-art. ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u24	Dear Vice President, thank you very much for your words. Ladies and gentlemen, honourable Members! At its 42nd regular meeting on 5 September this year, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia set out the text of the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Legal Protection in Public Procurement Procedures and sent it to the National Assembly for consideration. The bill before you sends some important solutions to our conviction. First of all, in view of the greater effectiveness of legal protection, this law no longer allows for the duplication of complaints in the same case and the introduction of new and new alleged infringements, even if the tenderer had known of them before. The applicant will not be able to state the same infringements repeatedly in the same procedure, neither in the repeated or continued procedure, where the continuation of a procedure is, of course, possible under the law, if the application has been withdrawn or has already been decided on; in particular, if he knew or could have known earlier when the first application was lodged. The bill also provides that only substantial infringements of the procedure, i.e. those which have an important and effective impact on public procurement procedures, will be assessed. The Government has also assessed that the deadlines to be taken into account by the State Audit Commission for the decision-making period are 15 days, with the possibility of an extension of an additional 15 days, in order to ensure the quality of the decision-making process. However, it has provided that complaints from larger projects, as is already the case with contracts co-financed by European funds, will be dealt with as a priority. It is also important to speed up the procedures that the Government has adopted, namely to set a time limit for the re-decision of the contracting authority after the State Audit Commission has returned the matter to the contracting authority for re-decision. Under the Public Procurement Act, it is stipulated that the contracting authority must take a decision on the contract within 90 days of receipt of the offer. However, if this decision becomes subject to review and is repealed by the State Audit Commission, the new deadline for the contracting authority has not been set at all; it is now 30 days. The new law also regulates the greater autonomy and expertise of the real protection body, i.e. the State Audit Commission. Since it is a body which has, in accordance with European Union law, the nature of the judicial body, it is right and it is essential that the appointment and discharge be arranged in a manner comparable to the appointment of judges. Therefore, a special commission consisting of members of the Council of Justice shall be established for the evaluation of the candidates and shall, on the basis of applications and interviews, verify the fulfilment of certain specific conditions such as professional and professional experience and personality. With this solution, the Government has ensured that the assessment of the fulfilment of the candidate's conditions will be professional and independent; however, the appointment of all qualified candidates, as is the case with judges, is still left to this assembly and to the competent working body. In addition, the conditions for appointment are increasing, candidates will need to be at least 35 years old, and have at least 9 years of experience in the field of public procurement. Finally, perhaps even more important, the law also introduces judicial protection. This is very important in order to ensure greater legal certainty and uniformity of decision-making. The Government shall, by means of a proposal for a law, regulate judicial protection by allowing an administrative dispute before the Administrative Court against the decision of the State Audit Commission. However, the action before the Administrative Court, which is possible within 30 days of the notification of the decisions of the State Audit Commission, does not have a suspensive effect in order to ensure speed and efficiency in the procurement proceedings, so it does not affect the concrete procedure, as this will not stop the proceedings; and the contracting authority may sign the contract. Finally, honourable Members and Members, I ask the National Assembly to carry out a third examination of the draft amendments to the Act at this meeting, in accordance with Rule 138 of the Rules of Procedure, in order to allow the proposed solutions to be implemented as soon as possible. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u25	Thank you very much. ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u26	Nice greetings to all present, colleagues, colleagues, distinguished minister! The amendment of the Law on Legal Protection in Public Procurement Procedures was discussed by the Committee on Home Affairs, Public Administration and Local Administration at its 12th meeting on 6 November this year. Within the time limit, the amendments were tabled by the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group to Articles 10 to 14 and Article 17 of the Act, and proposals for amendments to the Committee were also tabled on the proposal of the members of the Government Coalition's Group to Articles 1, 4, 11 and 14. The Minister of Public Administration presented the main objectives of the novel. He added that the Government is also preparing a novelty of the Public Procurement Act. The representative of the Legal and Legislative Service made comments on Articles 4, 14 and 20 of the draft law. The representative of the Council of State presented the Commission's opinion on State regulation, which supports the proposal for a law. The President of the State Audit Commission explained that they had been involved in amending the draft law. He stressed that in the field of public procurement, it is a major problem to highlight the lack of business and public procurement culture and ethics. In the debate, the representative of the Party's Group, Alenka Bratušek, stressed that the implementation of major infrastructure projects in Slovenia took an abnormally long time. She expressed the belief that the Supreme Court would be more appropriate for judicial protection than the Administrative Court. The representatives of the Group of the Parties of the Modern Centre expressed their conviction that judicial protection would not shorten the deadlines but would extend them; however, legal protection should be ensured and thus not reduce the efficiency of public procurement and ensure that public funds are used in accordance with the principles of legality, economy and transparency. They agreed with the representative of the SAB Group that legal protection should be within the purview of the Supreme Court and not the Administrative Court. The representatives of the Slovenian Democratic Party Group explained that the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group had in January this year called for an urgent meeting of the Commission of the National Assembly on the supervision of public finances on the revision of public procurement, which then adopted a decision inviting the Commission, inter alia, the Ministry of Public Administration to amend the area of public procurement, so that it would not be just the blue sky over the State Audit Commission. The new law brings some good solutions and unnecessarily complicates the procedure in the field of appointment and expands the composition of the State Audit Commission from 5 to 7 members without any reasonable reason. Another bad solution highlighted by the representative of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group is legal protection, which was also problematic by the representatives of the SAB and SMC. For this reason, the SDS Group has tabled amendments proposing that legal protection be provided by the Supreme Court and not by the Administrative Court. They also stressed that the proposed solution did not have a suspension effect and pointed out that an institution should be established to ensure that the procedure was finally resolved before parts of the project were started, otherwise compensation would be paid by taxpayers, which is irresponsible. The representative of the Left Group pointed out the necessity of changing business practices and cartel arrangements. It noted overpayments from public funds in all areas. In her opinion, the new law does not solve the problems, so the Left Group will not support them. The representative of the Social Democrats Group said that the draft law understood as an attempt to increase independence, efficiency and expertise in procurement procedures. The representatives of the Marjan Šarca Group pointed out that there is a public belief that everything is wrong in Slovenia in the field of public procurement; this is not yet the case, since only 2% of public procurement is decided by the State Audit Commission. Public procurement legislation is also not poor. The proposal for a law gives greater effect to the procedure; preventing duplication of audit requests will increase the speed of the procedure. At the conclusion of the debate, the Committee adopted amendments to Articles 1, 4, 11 and 40 of the Law and, at the proposal of the same parliamentary groups, amendments to the Committee's amendments to Article 14 of the Law. However, the Committee did not adopt any of the amendments of the Slovenian Democratic Party's Parliamentary Group, which were tabled under Articles 10, 12, 17 and have become irrelevant due to the non-admission of these amendments to Article 11 and the amendments of the SDS Group to Articles 11, 13 and 14 of the Act. Following the discussion, the Committee voted, in accordance with Rule 128 of the Rules of Procedure of the National Assembly, on all articles of the draft law together with 11 votes in favour and 3 against.

While in Austria we have contractors who will soon be in the middle of construction work, we are still dealing with who will receive this invitation to tender; let us ask ourselves if we are an effective country in this regard. We're not effective. This law is also ready to speed up procedures. What is crucial to us is that, in the case of the audit procedure in which DCOM annuls the contracting authority's decision, it sets a shorter period within which the contracting authority must take a new decision. The proposal reduces this to 60 days from the current 90 days. If it were in real, even greater interest, this 60 - day deadline could be reduced to 30 days; why it should be 60 days too, we could further speed up the procedures. But what we've got on the table is better than what's right now. Another important thing that we see as the objective of change is also an important increase in the expertise of the State Audit Commission and also of independence. Only appointment; if you look at how the members of the audit committee have in the past been intertwined with client interests, then you will quickly see that a system similar to the appointment of, for example, KPK and other similar institutions is a better solution than we have in existing legislation. But nothing will change without the responsibility of any individual, surely. Furthermore, the abuse of the right to legal protection and the so-called chain should be highlighted. Here we agree with the amendment on the Supreme Court by the Slovenian Democratic Party. In New Slovenia, we will support this legal protection amendment because it is an important element in the further exercise of responsibility. The law on the table is better than existing legislation and should be supported; with the inclusion of the amendment of the Slovenian Democratic Party, the law will be even better. It is not necessary that this law will not be effective, but it is the right step forward if we are to make more effective changes to public procurement legislation. In New Slovenia, we will support the bill.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u39	Thank you very much. Franc Kramar will present the position of the Party's Group, Alenka Bratušek.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u40	Thank you, Vice President. Your Honor! The main purpose and the most important objective of the changes proposed by the Government is to increase the efficiency of public procurement. In practice, as we are all aware from the current case of the construction of the second tube of the caravan tunnel, a number of shortcomings, particularly the inadmissible delay and delay in the construction of construction works in infrastructure projects, have been demonstrated. The government has therefore drawn up a proposal for amendments that would in practice eliminate systemic anomalies. The data show that, in less than 300 cases, of a total of around 6,000 public procurements, procedures are completed annually by an audit at the State Audit Commission. Half of 300 cases involve larger infrastructure projects with large amounts of tax money in play. As a result, there are delays in the execution of orders, as we may have witnessed in the case of the construction of the second track and the already mentioned Karavanka tunnel. In most cases, there is abuse of procedures by contracting authorities and tenderers, but unfortunately the current legislation permits such abuses. Complaints are particularly problematic. The amendment refers, inter alia, to the regulation of judicial protection against the decisions of the State Audit Commission. Slovenia is the only member of the European Union whose legislation does not provide for such judicial protection. The current law does not provide for regular or exceptional remedies, nor does it allow for an administrative dispute against the decision of the Commission. This is a sensitive area, as a number of questions are emerging whether this will contribute to a more efficient process; or whether it will only continue to worsen them. In the process of adopting the law, we have heard on several sides that the introduction of judicial protection will not help to carry out procedures faster, but even vice versa. We have also had such concerns in our parliamentary group, but the proponents have stressed that the initiation of the administrative dispute will not have a delayed effect and should therefore not have a significant impact on the extension of the proceedings. At least in the beginning, judicial protection may not significantly help to shorten proceedings, but judicial practice that will develop and contribute to greater legal certainty is crucial here. The Ministry, by means of a proposal for a law, has designated the Administrative Court as the holder of judicial proceedings against the decisions of the State Audit Commission. There was quite a dilemma on this account. We also in the Alenka Bratušek Group doubted that judicial protection before the Administrative Court would have a positive impact on the speedy course of proceedings. Indeed, the proceedings before this court in practice take about 500 days on average, which is absolutely too long. In this regard, proposals were made to perhaps make it more appropriate for the Supreme Court. At the meeting of the parent working body, a supplement was also adopted, setting a 90-day deadline in which the Administrative Court will have to draw up a decision. Human resources support is also envisaged, which we consider to be good. The proposal contains solutions that will contribute to greater independence and expertise of the State Audit Commission. The appointment of its members and the President of the Commission is changing, and the number of members from the existing 5 to 7 is also increasing. Conditions for appointment are tightened, which will contribute to greater expertise. Among other things, in the Alenka Bratušek Party, we are pleased to see a solution that foresees that the Commission will take priority over projects worth more than €10 million. In order to prevent the abuse of legal protection, it will be assessed only in the event of substantial infringements which have a real impact on the procurement process. Public procurement procedures in our country are really taking too long. As already mentioned, major infrastructure projects pose a particular problem, where procedures are not acceptable for years. In Slovenia, we need systemic solutions that will fully regulate the area of public procurement. In Alenka Bratušek we estimate that the longevity of the appeals procedures is one of the major obstacles to the faster implementation of large infrastructure projects. Therefore, we expect that the novels that are ahead of us today will remove this anomaly and help to speed up the launch of strategic projects such as the second line of Divaca-Koper, the third development axis and the second tube of the caravan tunnel. In the Alenka Bratušek Group, we will support the bill because we really want faster and more transparent procurement procedures, especially when infrastructure projects are important in the game for the country. We want the adoption of the proposal to do so to actually help.

The Law on Legal Protection in Public Procurement Procedures is practically a passenger to the basic Public Procurement Act, which regulates the legal protection of tenderers, contracting entities and public interest as such. This legal protection is regulated in the pre-audit procedure before the contracting authority, further in the audit procedure before the State Audit Commission for the revision of procurement procedures, and further in the judicial procedure itself. With the aim of effective legal protection against infringements in these proceedings, the Ministry has prepared a new amendment to the law. The two proposed solutions are key: the introduction of full judicial protection against the decisions of the State Audit Commission in the form of an administrative dispute and, secondly, an increase in the independence and expertise of the State Audit Commission. With regard to the envisaged possibility of initiating an administrative dispute, it should be pointed out that it is not allowed for low-value public procurement and for tendering procedures following the previous publication. The right to judicial protection is also limited by the fact that the action can only require a determination of the illegality of the decision of the State Audit Commission, but not perhaps its removal, amendment or even reimbursement of costs. In this part, some lawyers dealing with procurement procedures are in doubt and have expressed concerns. With regard to the envisaged new procedure for the appointment of seven members of the State Audit Commission, we also raised concerns in the Desus Group during the drafting of the text of the law; some of these were included in the amended bill. However, I still raise the question at this point as to whether the proposal will really lead to greater independence and expertise for the members of the Commission. In our opinion, the independence and expertise of the members of this commission should not be questionable at all. However, an indication of the political nature of the appointment of members will be present for any person who will be appointed or loved in the National Assembly. At the same time, we expect the Ministry to, in the event that these concerns are later found to be justified in practice, approach to the development of new solutions as soon as possible. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u52	Thank you, Vice President, for your word. I was already on the committee when we discussed this novel, and I said one thing that I find extremely interesting, and I will repeat it again today. My friend Möderndorfer had a slightly different percentage here, and I got a number from the Ministry, so I believe her. We need to know that such public procurements, which are of higher value, are multimillion-dollar public infrastructure contracts. It is normal that the behaviour of tenderers in such public contracts is different from that of public contracts. I want to say that in our country, the legislation governing public procurement is good. She's not bad. The Public Procurement Act, which, despite its many changes, we know that we already have a ZJN-3, is still viable and is also compatible with the Law on Legal Protection in Public Procurement Procedures. This, as has already been said, confirms the fact that we have a very small percentage of such public contracts ending before the State Audit Commission. As far as this additional legal protection is concerned, which is being dealt with and regulated under this amendment, this seems to be a positive gain, because this legal protection is not only welcome from the point of view of the tenderers who have this additional legal protection, but is welcome from a broader perspective, which I have already explained on the Committee. The decision of the Administrative Court under this legal protection will allow the establishment of a single case-law which will be welcome not only for the tenderers, as I said, but also for the contracting authorities which will take into account the uniform case-law already in the preparation of the tender documents, then in the decision-making process of the pre-audit procedure and, last but not least, for the State Audit Board when it decides in the audit procedure. In the context of what I have said, there is also the fact that the positive effect of such an arrangement has already been shown abroad. A smaller number of audits have been submitted. As has already been said, when a uniform case law is established, one and the same errors are not repeated. Why the Administrative Court and not the Supreme Court? Here, I understand, the argument is quite simple. This decision has been taken in cooperation with the Ministry of Justice and must be based on the role played by the Supreme Court in our judicial system. Its role is primarily to harmonise the case-law of higher courts, not to rule in individual cases and in substantive disputes. I have also said this to the Committee on Home Affairs, and I will point out once again that with the latest amendment to the Law on Courts, such powers have been conferred on the Court of Justice, according to its substantive ruling, the Supreme Court has been seized and transferred to the Administrative Court. That is why the regulation we have before us today, as proposed by the Ministry of Public Administration, is, in my opinion, acceptable and appropriate. In this light, talking about some referrals to re-decision and how this would delay these procurement procedures is a bit misleading, as the regulation proposed will not affect the award of a public contract, since the Administrative Court will not make a substantive decision on the award or decision of the tenderer or contracting authority, but will only establish the judgement of the Administrative Court, which means that it will not affect the decision taken by the State Audit Commission and will become legally and definitive. Let me also explain and comment on the findings of some high damages. As I said, the judgement of the Administrative Court will be found, which does not mean automatic compensation, without prejudice to any liability of the contracting authority that it has caused the tenderer property damage. In any event, the tenderer will have to file a civil action, have to prove that he suffered business damage, a causal link between the conduct of the contracting authority, and prove that he was in that group of tenderers, or that he was the one who should have received that public order, and that he suffered some business damage. The provider will have to make a number of efforts to prove in civil proceedings some compensation that belongs to him. Fear of the extension of public procurement procedures — this time component between the Administrative Court and the Supreme Court is not exactly comparable, as we must be aware that proceedings before the Administrative Court require a slightly different procedure, have a finding procedure and a hearing of witnesses with other procedural activities, which of course prolongs the proceedings before the Administrative Court. However, I do not wish to be a lawyer of the Administrative Court, but it is important to be aware that the proceedings before the Supreme Court take place significantly differently, especially on the basis of the status of the file; and there are no other procedural acts that would prolong this proceedings before the Supreme Court. So this comparison is like comparing pears with apples. I've already said about the damage, what I thought should be stressed. This concludes this agenda item. We move on to agenda item 11 – SECOND REVELATION OF THE PROPOSAL OF A LAW ON THE PAYMENT OF A NON-PAYED SUPPORT FOR THE CHILD IN THE FIELD OF THE REGULAR PROCEDURE. The draft law was tabled by the Government for consideration by the National Assembly. To further explain the draft law, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, Urban Krajcar, State Secretary at the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u100	Dear President, honourable Members, honourable Members! Today we have before us the bill on payment of the unpaid child care allowance. Initially, the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities in December 2018 joined the problem of parents of blind and visually impaired children. It is for parents who have, since 2003, continued to choose between two supplements, namely, the allowance for assistance and service under the law governing pension and disability insurance, and the allowance for the care of the child under the law governing parental protection and family benefits. The proposal for the law in question covers two groups of persons. In addition to the blind and visually impaired children, the law also includes children with moderate, severe or severe mental impairment who have continued education in a special programme after their full age. Since 1 January 2003, the Retirement and Disability Insurance Act has been laid down in such a way as to enable the beneficiary of a blind or visually impaired child to enjoy only one right, either an allowance for the care of the child or an allowance for assistance and service. On the basis of such interpretation, which was exempted by the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, the social work centres did not recognise an allowance for the care of the child if the beneficiary received an aid and service allowance. In 2012, the Supreme Labour and Social Court ruled that the beneficiary of the allowance for the assistance and care of children, while the beneficiary of the allowance for the care of the child is the parent of that child. On the basis of the above, these additives shall not be excluded from one another. Then, in June 2015, the Ministry sent a letter to all social work centres that parents and children could apply both supplements. The second group of children governed by the proposal of the law are those who have been educated in the specific programme. In the then position of the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport, persons with moderate, severe or severe mental impairment after 18 years of age could continue their education in a special programme up to 26 years of age, but in this way they did not retain student status. The student status, however, is a condition for the acquisition and entitlement to an allowance for the care of the child. In 2015, the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport changed its position and decided that children who are educated in specific tailor-made programmes also have the status of education and thus have the right to an allowance for the care of the child. The draft law thus constitutes the legal basis for payment of the child care allowance, which should be paid in 2003 until the end of 2015 and was not due to the interpretation at the time. If I conclude, the proposal allows beneficiaries to repay the child's outstanding allowances from 2003 onwards, while at the same time giving the law the right to enforce the reimbursement of the judicial and enforcement costs incurred by the beneficiaries in actions against the State. At the same time, I would also like to express my gratitude for the positive parliamentary debate that has helped to ensure that the law is likely to be adopted, which is of particular interest to us at the Ministry. Thank you all very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u101	Thank you very much. The draft law was considered by the Labour Committee, the family, social affairs and persons with disabilities as a working mother. I give the floor to President Vojk Starović for the presentation of the report of the Committee. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u102	Thank you for your word, chairman. Your Honor! The Committee on Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Disabled Persons, as a parent, considered the proposal for a bill on the payment of an unpaid allowance for the care of the child submitted to the National Assembly by the Government with a proposal for a hearing and adoption following a regular procedure. Representatives of the proposer with the Minister for Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities, representative of the Legislative and Legal Service, representative of the State Council, representative of the injured parents and other injured parents and representative of the Social Chamber of Slovenia participated in the work of the Committee. The representative of the proposer presented the novel. The President of the Council of State presented the opinion and said that the Commission of the Council of State had endorsed the bill. The representative of the Legislative and Legal Service said that the opinion made comments mainly of a legislative and technical nature and had been duly taken into account by the amendments tabled by the coalition groups in the vast majority. The question remains, however, whether all beneficiaries who have been forced to choose only one of the two supplements have chosen an aid and service allowance and, if not, how to regulate the situation of those who have received a child-care allowance all the time have not received an aid and service allowance. In the debate, all the debaters agreed that children and their parents should finally be corrected for the injustices they had long been alerting them to. This also raised the question of who was responsible for the situation and why it took so long to correct those injustices. The issue has also been addressed twice by the Commission for petitions, human rights and equal opportunities, which it has sought to solve as soon as possible. The Minister said that, in order to find a solution and help the parents, she had convened a meeting with the State Attorney's Office and the Ombudsman, where a legal basis had been sought for the preparation of a special law. The representative of the injured parents said that the initiatives and requests for resolution of the problem had been addressed to all possible addresses, but no one had taken them into account, except for the current Minister of Labour and the Commission for Petitions. It was pointed out that the obligation to reimburse costs was due to the incorrect interpretation of the eligibility of the allowance for aid and service and the allowance for the care of the child. The law is in fact due to the decision of the Higher Labour and Social Court in Ljubljana, which ruled that the right to an allowance for the care of a child under the Parental Protection and Family Benefits Act and the right to an allowance for assistance and service under the applicable laws were not excluded. The beneficiaries of the payment will, on the basis of a proposal for a law, both those who have exercised the right and those who have not fulfilled the conditions. The following raised the question of what is the amount of interest to be included in the debaters' opinion. The minister said that they preferred interest to take into account today's amount of allowance, because they believe that this will give parents more. On the question of what will happen to parents who will not submit their application, the Minister explained that they do not have an official record, so it is not possible to deal with the matter as opposed to the party's role. She stressed, however, that through various campaigns they would ensure that damaged parents were informed. The Committee adopted the amendments tabled. A complementary bill has been prepared. The amendments for the meeting of the National Assembly were not tabled. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u106	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues! However, and I must point out this, today, in addition to the above, I rightly ask myself what is wrong with other children who also deserve both supplements; both the allowance for help and service and the allowance for the care of the child. I already addressed to the Minister for Labour, Family and Social Affairs and equal opportunities this March and later in August the question or initiative, what about the other children, those who need 24-hour special care and protection: disabled persons, deaf people, tetraplegics and so on. These children, unlike the blind and visually impaired after the ZPIZ-2, are not entitled to either the aid allowance or the one for the service, but they also need 24-hour care or care. The fact is, if they live at home and not in the institution, they are cared for by their parents, often without any help. The Personal Assistance Act, which entered into force at the beginning of this year, also provides that only those who have reached the age of 18 are entitled to personal assistance. Which means that minors with disabilities and children simply do not have the right to personal assistance or are not legally entitled to it. There are also many parents who cannot afford to help themselves because they cannot pay someone to care for and care for their child, which is why they are not left alone. Especially should it be difficult for single - parent families. These children and their parents would still need an allowance for help and service, or even more, the right to an allowance for help and service is a personal right that should belong to these children. In its 2012 judgment, the Higher Labour and Social Court stated that the right to an allowance for a child is the right of parents to cover increased costs of living. The right to an allowance for assistance and service is a personal right, the right of the child. The Court therefore made it clear that there were two rights which were not mutually exclusive. Dearly beloved, it is important for all these severely ill children and their parents to provide adequate financial assistance and support to the greatest extent possible. But not only financial assistance, but also support in all other areas – socialisation, the appropriate involvement of these children and their parents in society so that they do not remain alone. These children must also be adequately involved in the work environment and adapted jobs when they grow up and if they are able and want to do so. We need to provide these children with new technological solutions and appropriate tools that, for example, enable the blind and visually impaired to work properly. In my view, these children are unjustifiably discriminated against and should be fully granted the right to an allowance for assistance and service and to care for the child in the same way as we recognize blind and visually impaired children. Protection and assistance to vulnerable groups are one of the fundamental elements of the social state. The main task of the country is to provide its people, especially their children, with opportunities for a decent life. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u114	Thank you, Chairman. Your Honor! The bill solves the problem of unpaid child care supplements and of helping and serving parents of blind and visually impaired children. In 2003, the novelty of the pension law came into force, on the basis of which the social work centres asked the parents to choose between the child care allowance and the allowance for assistance and service. Most parents have decided on the latter. Nevertheless, some social work centres paid both allowances to their parents, and the State later claimed that the allowances received were returned as a creditor to their parents. The decision of one of the parents to try to achieve her right in court was prompted by the decision of the Supreme Labour and Social Court in 2012. This has ruled that the right to an allowance for the care of a child is the right of parents and of an indirect child who needs special care and protection. On the other hand, the right to an allowance for assistance and service is a personal right acquired by blind persons, i.e. children. By 2015 there was also a different interpretation of Article 75a of the Basic School Act and the associated definition of student status, which, in the case of a person with moderate, severe or severe mental impairment, serves as a basis for the exercise of the right to an allowance for the care of the child. By 2015, the above mentioned persons at the age of 18 were considered to have lost their student status despite continuing their education in a specific programme, which is possible until the age of 26. Since then, they have been able to exercise rights under the Social Protection Act of mentally and physically disabled persons. That same year, the Ministry of Education, Science and Sport took the view that, under the Basic School Act, it was possible to conclude that the legislator had foreseen student status during the extended period of education. As a result, persons with moderate, severe or severe mental impairment are also between the ages of 18 and 26 if they continue education in a specific programme entitled to an allowance for the care of the child. All the above and many discussions and discussions between the policy and the various stakeholders ultimately led to the point where we are today. As has already been said, the bill regulates the right to payment of unpaid child care allowances and the reimbursement of the costs incurred by parents in determining the State's liability for the unpaid child care allowance. In addition, the State is obliged to pay the obligations arising from the applicable legislation. According to the proposal of the law, the beneficiaries will be both parents of children who have exercised the right to supplement and those who have not. The overall financial implications will amount to a good EUR 5 million and the resources are already provided in the budget. In the Parliamentary Group of the Party Alenka Bratušek we support the proposed law. In doing so, we stress the fact that the money paid will not be able to erase all the hardships that parents felt due to the misinterpretation of the law and the slow functioning of the state apparatus. In order to avoid such and similar cases in the future, we need to manage state institutions more effectively and develop better quality public policies that will respond more quickly to change. In doing so, we must not neglect taking responsibility for the decisions taken. By strengthening the relationship between citizens and institutions, we also strengthen the confidence of citizens and citizens in public systems and services. But trust is the foundation of democracy and also what helps to preserve it.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u122	Madam President, thank you so much for your words. Dear Members, honourable Members, representatives of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia! On 22 October this year, the National Assembly adopted the Law on the Judicial and Out-of-court Protection of Former Holders of Qualified Obligations of Banks, which was tabled by the Government of the Republic of Slovenia in the legislative procedure and whose purpose is to implement Constitutional Court Decision No 295/13, which has been delayed by the legislator for several years. The Constitutional Court’s decision is a result of the State Council’s request to initiate a procedure to assess the constitutionality of certain articles in the Banking Act, since the State Council has been working since 2013 on the issue of the declassification of former holders of qualified obligations by banks, with its observations, views and opinions also actively involved in the examination of that law in the working bodies of the National Assembly, with a view to achieving the real implementation of that decision by the Constitutional Court. In the State Council, we believe that the legal solutions adopted not only eliminate the unconstitutionalities found but can also lead to possible new unconstitutionalities. In this regard, we note in particular the concerns regarding: the exclusive competence of the courts to try disputes under the said law, the issues related to the determination of judicial fees, the application of procedures for judging economic disputes, the protection of the personal data of the defendants and, last but not least, the out-of-court payment of lump sums to certain former holders of qualified liabilities of banks. Those concerns, in the opinion of the Council of State, may lead to violations of the right to effective judicial protection, violations of the principle of equality, the principle of equal protection of rights, information privacy and the right to free economic initiative. At the same time, we note a number of concerns, which are largely related to the concerns of the State Council and which have been raised by the public concerned. Also, the letters of the Supreme Court and the Court of Justice in Maribor show that the law is inconsistent with the judiciary, as they point out in their letters that the implementation of the law will result in a heavy burden on the judiciary as a whole. In the State Council, we are of the view that the law does not ensure the proper implementation of the Constitutional Court's decision, as it does not provide effective judicial protection for former holders of qualified liabilities of banks. We also believe that the adoption of additional new legislation to address the problems of this law does not constitute an appropriate legal solution. We therefore propose that the National Assembly reconsider the legal solutions adopted. Thank you very much.

It also explained that the Council of State considers that the proposal for a law on judicial protection of former holders of qualified liabilities by banks is not properly regulated. He stressed that the purpose of the proposal for a law is to regulate an effective judicial procedure for the protection of former holders of the obligations of banks and shareholders, not to establish political or criminal liability. In its written opinion, the representative of the Legislative and Legal Service concluded that the statements of the Council of State coincided with a written opinion on the draft law of 14 June this year and a written opinion on certain amendments of 6 September this year. It was pointed out in the debate that delays in regulating the area could also lead to State compensation. As regards the exclusive jurisdiction of the Maribor District Court, it was explained that it was proposed by the Supreme Court of the Republic of Slovenia. However, care for the smooth functioning of the judiciary must be primarily in the domain of the judiciary, with some difficulties encountered in the coordination process. It was also pointed out that all the concerns of the Council of State had already been answered in the process so far. In view of possible constitutionally controversial solutions, it was explained in the debate that the law governs a specific type of responsibility which has not yet been decided by the Constitutional Court. With regard to the protection of the personal data of the defendants, it was pointed out that the high amounts showed a public interest, as it was necessary to determine who could have obtained it. Following the discussion, the Committee adopted the following opinion by a majority of all members, 10 votes in favour and 5 against: The Law on the Judicial and Out-of-Court Protection of Former Holders of Qualified Obligations of Banks is appropriate. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u125	Thank you very much. In order to explain my opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government to Mr Methoda Draganji, State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u126	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Dear Members, distinguished representative of the Council of State! As a motion for a postponement veto, the Council of State stated five substantive reasons for the State Assembly to reconsider the decisions on the draft law tabled by the Government in the legislative procedure. Responses to the concerns of the State Council were formulated by the Government in a written response of 7. 11. 2019. In particular, the Government rejects one of the key indications of the State Council that the deletions carried out constituted a retroactive intervention into the property rights of deleted holders. The Constitutional Court has determined that the expectations of a subordinated bank investor to be paid from public funds were not legally protected by expectations. The basic purpose and purpose of the law, as we have repeatedly stressed, is to ensure judicial protection for former holders of subordinated bonds and shareholders of banks who shared the burden with the State in the bank recovery process. The absence of effective judicial protection due to the deletions imposed by Banka Slovenije has been a major problem since the adoption of the Constitutional Decision in 2016. Members and Members have the opportunity to provide judicial protection to former investors by confirming this law. Otherwise, we can expect the decision of the European Court of Human Rights to be taken by some of the affected investors. The decision of the ECHR shall be unpredictable and may extend from the amounts for equitable satisfaction and for property and intangible damage to the general judgment in the direction of adoption or amendment of legislation. This means that we can repeat Alešić's judgment against the Republic of Slovenia on foreign exchange deposits of Croatian and Bosnian savers, which has had major public financial consequences for Slovenia. In the opinion of the Government, the State Council puts forward provisions that would be of great benefit to deleted investors and thus, in our opinion, seek to pursue the partial interests of certain interest groups that have exerted pressure both in public treatment with their public publications and with extensive lobbying. The law you are re-deciding today does not interfere retroactively with the regime at the time of the disappearance, which is the key expectation of opponents of this law. The Bank of Slovenia's warnings of monetary financing and interference of the independence of the central bank and the related findings of the non-constitutional nature of these provisions were answered in a reasoned manner. By this law, Banka Slovenije does not assume any obligations of the State. In this law, we consistently apply the principle of Banka Slovenije's responsibility for implementing extraordinary measures in 2013 and 2014. This form of responsibility was imposed by the Constitutional Court in its 124th recital to the constitutional provision, which states: “The confidence of investors present and future in the reasonable security of investments in qualified liabilities of banks can only create an arrangement whereby the Bank of Slovenia will have to demonstrate why the measure affecting investors was a case of necessity.” (Final reference) In its 120th recital, the Constitutional Court stated that in this case it was the Bank of Slovenia's specially regulated liability for compensation. The law follows consistently the Constitution of 2016, so we ask that Members and Members, by re-confirming this law, be provided with legal protection imposed by the Constitutional Court. This would prove that we are acting as a mature country and provide reasonable protection for investors also in more risky financial investments. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u127	Thank you very much. I now give the floor to the representatives of the groups of Members. Ms Jani Prednik will present the position of the SD Group. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u128	Dear Vice-President, distinguished representatives of the Government, Representative of the Council of State! Today, at the request of the National Council, we are once again deciding on the approval of the draft law, the sole purpose of which is to provide judicial protection to all those who, due to the actions of the Bank of Slovenia, had to participate in the rehabilitation of banks and lost a large majority of the funds they had invested in the so-called subordinated bonds of banks. The law was drafted and adopted because the Constitutional Court of 2016 ruled that the Bank of Slovenia's actions were lawful and consistent with the Constitution, but that the holders of the qualifying obligations were breached by the fact that they were not given the opportunity to challenge the decisions of the bank regulator before the courts, since the injured persons did not have access to the data on which they could draw up the action, nor were the procedural possibilities to bring an action for the protection of their rights. The law, which we have to rule on today, allows and gives subordinates the opportunity to seek their right before the courts. The Council of State, in its request for re-decision, points out a series of indications that it considers that the law should not be enforced. Among other issues are the jurisdiction of the courts, access to data, rules for the management of disputes, the public disclosure of the data of the defendants and the absence of compensation for certain damages, which is why the Council of State considers that the adoption of law does not provide effective judicial protection to former holders. Of course, the expectations of some for this law are high. Some believe that the law should return all their contributions to the injured, as it is intended to go to a bank recovery for crime directed from abroad, others again feel that the state acted appropriately when the banks were being rehabilitated. Thirdly, they claim that there were no grounds for recovery at all. But this law only regulates the right of injured persons to seek their right in court. And this is what this law regulates; it gives victims access to data and the possibility to file and bring lawsuits before a competent court in Maribor, which, due to the occupation of other courts and the complexity of proceedings, is suggested by the judicial branch of the authorities. The State Council’s request also underlines the absence of a return on funds lost to certain investors and several times exposes the disproportionately strong position of the bank regulator compared to investors, overlooking the fact that this law was complemented by the possibility of being settled with the smallest investors, who, in view of their material situation, can reasonably be assumed to have not cooperated with speculative intent when purchasing securities. However, it is by no means incumbent on the National Assembly to adopt a law to repay all the funds invested to investors, since the Constitutional Court has clearly ruled that the measures were legally and constitutionally compatible. Moreover, the Court has made it clear that the Constitution does not give rise to the obligation of the State to reimburse an individual for investments that have proved to be economically unsuccessful. For all of this, we believe that we have an appropriate law before us, which will finally guarantee the right to judicial protection, and that is why we will also support the law in re-decision in the Group of Social Democrats.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u136	Thank you, Vice President. Dear Members and Members! The Constitutional Court ruled in 2016 that the measure of so-called deletion of subordinated Slovenian banks bonds in 2013 was not unconstitutional. However, it has been decided that due legal protection should be provided by law in the light of the specificity of this measure. And just as a court order is being ordered, it is referring to the government's proposal for a law that is back here before us. On 22 October this year, at the 12th meeting of the National Assembly, we decided on this law for the third time and also adopted it. On the other hand, the Council of State has assessed that the law of the legal proceedings is not regulated in an appropriate manner. In particular, they raised concerns about the exclusive competence of the courts to judge in such disputes and to determine court fees. In their view, it is also problematic to apply the procedures for judging economic disputes mainly because of the imbalance between the parties' situation. The Council of State draws attention to the contention of provisions providing for the public hearing of the data on the complainants. They consider that the legislator did not sufficiently justify the proportionality of the proposed measures, which constitute an infringement of constitutional rights. We in the SAB Party remain firmly convinced that it is in the public interest to publish the applicants, as it will be definitively revealed who the actual holders of the qualified liabilities of the banks were. Here I aim primarily at foreign owners, because their pressures are the strongest. Another time. In our parliamentary group, we have advocated a special settlement for socially weaker investors during the entire legislative process, namely for natural persons whose income did not exceed the average annual gross salary in the country at the time of the investment. In the second reading, it was thanks to the SAB Party that part of the law became a solution offering the possibility of a special out-of-court settlement for those investors who are in a socially weaker situation. For those who have invested their savings in good faith, it is a safe investment and who cannot afford long-term and expensive judicial proceedings or even want such proceedings. These natural persons have an immediate payment of a lump sum of 80 % of the value of the instrument of the second and third lines paid at the time of purchase, but not more than 20 000 euros for each order. The amount of EUR 20 000 is determined on the basis of the average annual gross salary in 2013 and is considered to be an appropriate social adjustment. The flat-rate compensation thus does not constitute a classic compensation or recognition of the claim on the basis, but rather a correction to address the social consequences intended to remedy the deterioration of the social situation of natural persons. Natural persons are not entities of competition law dealing with economic activity and therefore the proposed regime does not constitute a risk of unlawful State aid. This is also what the European Commission has found in the case of Italy. However, the fact is that the special settlement scheme is not intended for so-called large investors who, by purchasing subordinated bonds, speculated and expected the State to save the bank's tax money and at least double their investment. With a special out-of-court settlement scheme, we would contribute to a faster and more efficient resolution of judicial disputes, thus also restoring confidence in the Slovenian financial system for citizens and citizens. The third essential solution for which the SAB Group has been committed from the outset is the limitation of the compensation on the purchase of the value of the financial instruments and, as this is a matter of compensation, the amount of the compensation must not exceed the damage suffered by the individual denominators of the subordinated bonds. In the SAB Group, we also stand today in the position we have advocated throughout the legislative process, we support the bill unanimously and thank once again, on behalf of small natural investors, all Members and Members who will do the same.
