The decommissioning of holders of bonds and shares in the rehabilitation of Slovenian banks in 2013 and 2014 is indeed a problem of the political and economic sovereignty of the Republic of Slovenia within the framework of the European Union. The political framework of the European Union is reflected through a legal assessment of whether the opinions of the European Commission are a binding legal source for Slovenia. In August 2013, the European Commission adopted State aid rules for banks where the responsibility for the rehabilitation of banks is not taken over by the central bank as the last lender, nor by the state, but primarily by the owners. subordinated debts must be written off or converted into equity shares. In autumn 2013, this recommendation became an integral part of the Banking Act. We got a disputed delete. At the time it was considered a recommendation, today it is a system of operation of the Banking Union. Two matters are important in the rehabilitation of the Slovenian banking system. Firstly, in its communications on banking and State aid procedures alone, the European Commission has exceeded the competences of the European Treaties and has therefore demanded too much in the wrong way. Secondly, whether the Slovenian political and monetary authorities have resorted to excessive measures to ensure the stability of the banking system, resorted to controversial retroactivity and inadmissibly intervened in private property. The constitutionality assessment procedure concerned a decision to terminate the obligation of banks to its committees in special circumstances and in the event of exceptional measures by the Bank of Slovenia. This measure does not provide effective judicial protection for deletions and there is no real possibility of complaints against the decisions and actions of the Bank of Slovenia. The rule of law is crucial here. The Constitutional Court defended, on behalf of the protection of the rights of the released holders of qualified obligations. The crucial issue is actually legitimacy, economic, political, procedural, imposed European solutions, assessing the superiority of the rules of the legal and political responsibility for the consequences of certain decisions of the European Commission and the European Central Bank. In the event of the resolution of the banking crisis, the fate of the deleted is important, but the defence of Slovenian economic sovereignty, the fundamental postulates of the market economy, such as property and bond relations, is crucial. The legalisation of judicial protection proceedings for former holders of qualified liabilities by banks is the fundamental purpose of the legislative proposal, which we are re-declaring today. The provision of a full overview of the instruments relating to the deletion relates to the valuation of the value of banks, the valuations of the value of banks' assets, the results of the asset quality review and the results of the tests of resistance to macroeconomic shocks for each bank. On the basis of the examinations made, sufficient time is provided for the preparation of the application. This will result in a point where we have a well-formed opinion on alternatives and find ourselves ahead of their choice or decision. From here on out, we take responsibility for decision-makers. The mystery we are facing is due to the unprecedented transfer of sovereignty between the country and the European Union, but the very Slovenian deletion of subordinates is proof of how dangerously we have missed the assessment of what it is and what the European Union can do. Therefore, the responsibility of Slovenian policy is indisputable and our imperative is to allow the adoption of the law and its application. ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u146	Dear Vice-President of the National Assembly, thank you very much for your words. On 22 October this year, the National Assembly adopted the Act amending the Law on the Balance of Public Finance, which was submitted to the legislative procedure by the Group of Members and Members with the first-signed Mr Miho Kordis. On 28 October this year, the Council of State adopted a request for the National Assembly to decide again on the law. In its request, the Council of State draws attention mainly to two, the first of which was the adoption of the law, since it was adopted without the participation of the social partners, which, within the framework of the Economic and Social Council, deal with issues and measures related to economic and social policy. In the context of such a debate, the social partners could carry out a comprehensive analysis of the student work within the framework of the current legislation and, through an assessment of the future effects of the proposed legislative changes, also assess the actual effects on the economy and the wider society. However, the second warning refers to the amount of the minimum hourly item which shall not be less than the hourly item calculated from the minimum wage for the performance of temporary and occasional works. The Council of State considers that the established basis for calculating the minimum hourly rate is disputed because it is identical to the worker’s hourly position with minimum wage, although it is different levels of competence for work. The State Council also points out the danger that more than 10% of the increase in the hourly rate for the performance of temporary and occasional works will be a heavy burden on the economy, which will consequently negatively affect the scope of such work, i.e. the extent of the work carried out by students and students, and thus the acquisition of first-time work experience. Equalisation of the hourly position for the performance of temporary and temporary work with the hourly position of the employee with minimum wage on the other hand will have a disstimulating effect on regular employees who know the work process and who are more effective in their work. The Council of State considers those arguments to be sufficiently valid for the National Assembly to reconsider the legal provisions adopted. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u150	Today we are again deciding on the proposal of the Left to improve the material position of students and students who are doing student work. The main guide of our proposal was to align the minimum hourly rate with the minimum wage and thus to pay decent student workers so that they can work less and study more. The minimum payment, as currently laid down for students, is not fair. The minimum hourly rate for student work is set at a lower rate than the estimated hour of employment for the minimum wage, thus violating the principle of equal pay for equal work. In addition, employers are not obliged to grant students any rights other than those of the employees. In this respect, it is worth noting that the student’s work differs from regular work only in its many apparent temporary and occasional nature, while all other elements of work, involvement in the organised work process, personal performance according to the employer’s instructions and supervision, are still present in the students. Also, the hourly rate is far behind for minimum wage growth. The minimum hourly rate has risen only for the poor 39 cents and 8.6% since 2015 when the minimum rate for students has been introduced, while the minimum wage has risen by more than 12%. That is why we have proposed in Levica, on the basis of an agreement with the Government of Marjana Sarca, that students should receive at least EUR 5 net for their work, since we also included in this amount the costs associated with the work recognised by the existing legislation, the costs for which we agreed in the coalition agreement to recognise them. But the coalition withdrew from this part of the agreement and also formally admitted that it did not intend to respect it. Despite the lack of respect already agreed on in Levica, we have agreed to another compromise. With the coalition, we have decided that the minimum hourly rate with the new year will rise to 4.76 euros net and will coordinate from there with the minimum wage. This Decision exists in writing, in the minutes of the meeting with the coalition held on 6 September this year. In Levica, we were prepared to talk and talk and seek compromises, and not, as the coalition likes to say, to force, condition, compromise and not know what else. However, in this liberal coalition, you can rely on only one fact that promises, agreements, conclusions and commitments mean nothing. In this case, the coalition also swallowed the word and, on the proposal of the Left, voted in addition to raising the minimum item not to €5 as proposed, not to €4.76, as agreed and agreed in the second compromise, but to the final €4.56 per hour. Here it enters the picture of the State Council, which even this relatively small lift would take to the students. It is clear in this regard, in the interest of whom he voted vetoly. Not in the interests of students, but in the exclusive interest of those employers who rely on the exploitation of the student workforce on behalf of and for the interest of capital. The block of student salaries was forced by the same people who last year opposed to raising the minimum wage, people who, at every increase in working rights, at every improvement in working conditions and at every rise in working wages in the defence of their own, put profit interests on their last legs. I really hope that in the second vote you will vote that the minimum hourly rate is at least slightly improved and that it starts to be aligned with the minimum wage growth. That's the least we can do today. In the future, however, by increasing the budget resources, we will have to provide all the socially weaker students with decent scholarships and accommodation opportunities. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u152	Honourable President, Honourable Members, Members, Representative of the Council of State! In the National Assembly today, you are again deciding on the Law on Amendments to the Law on Public Finance Balance in Work concerning the work of students and students. In opening, I would first like to give a few words to the social dialogue. In this regard, a number of observations have been made in the legislative process. In this regard, it is certainly necessary to clarify that the Government of the Republic of Slovenia addressed to the Economic and Social Council the request for a presentation of the solutions included in the proposal and the views of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia in the legislative process, which would allow for an appropriate debate on this important topic. As the rules of the Economic and Social Council did not provide for the consideration of proposals for amendments to legislation that were not governmental, the Government proceeded to initiate a procedure to amend the rules on the functioning of the Economic and Social Council. The amended rules were solemnly signed last week and provide that the Economic and Social Council will be able to consider in the future proposals for amendments to the legislation to be tabled by other eligible applicants. Together with the agreement to amend the rules on the functioning of the Economic and Social Council, we have also agreed on the conclusion of the Protocol of the Government and the social partners on respect for and promotion of social dialogue. The protocol was also solemnly signed last week. Under the Protocol, an agreement has been established that the Government will consistently respect and implement the applicable rules on the functioning of the Economic and Social Council and, without prior substantive consideration of the social partners in the Economic and Social Council, will not adopt or propose to adopt rules which fall within the fundamental economic and social fields which the Economic and Social Council is considering in accordance with its rules. As regards the legal proposal, it has been repeatedly explained in the regular, preliminary legislative procedure concerning the diversity of legal bases and also the different nature of the part of students and students themselves that the purpose of temporary and occasional work of students and students is to enable them to gain work experience and additional earnings for students and students during education and for employers to select potential future staff. We also stress the temporary and occasional nature of the work of students and students, so it is work that is done for a certain period of time or occasionally. The draft law was amended in the procedure, i.e. in the calculation of the minimum hourly rate, a standard of average number of monthly working hours was applied, i.e. 174 hours. This is a solution that takes into account the Government's previous comments. The proposal was also amended in the work setting out the additions to the minimum hourly heading by deleting the allowances in the proposal, since it is an essential element which distinguishes student work from the working relationship, its temporaryity or occasionality. However, the allowances resulting from the specific allocation of working time shall only be paid to the worker in accordance with the working law when the worker is exposed to specific conditions of work. Students and students do not carry out their work on the same legal basis as workers and consequently not under the same circumstances, and such a change in the proposal was considered appropriate by the Government. The novel also foresees a link to the growth of the minimum wage, which, as in the case of temporary and occasional part of pensioners, is considered appropriate. Your Honor! The proposal you confirm today has been largely amended in the legislative process in accordance with the comments or recommendations made by the Government and is therefore not opposed by the Government. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u162	Thank you for your word, Madam Vice President. Colleagues and colleagues, dear guests on the balcony! With the bill today we will redefine the minimum hourly rate for the work of students and students. At the end of 2014, the minimum hourly rate was fixed by dividing the gross salary of EUR 790 by the average monthly working hours of 174 hours. Thus, the legal rate of EUR 4.5 per hour was then determined. It was also decided that the level of the minimum gross hourly rate was to be aligned with the movement of the average salary paid for the period from January to December of the previous year, compared to the average salary paid for the same period of the year before that. Once a year, it shall be appointed by the Minister responsible for the work and no later than 1 April of the current calendar year. This was the last time that the minimum hourly position for the work of students and students was harmonised with 1 April 2019. In the Desus Group, we believe that the proposed solutions to the law followed the concept of “Every work counts”. In the past, a step has been taken by loading student work with contributions to health and pension insurance and at the same time setting a minimum hourly rate of €4.5. This happened at the end of 2014. The current proposal for provisions of the law will allow for the establishment of a minimum hourly rate for the work of pupils and students at the level of 5.4 euros, according to 4.89 euros, effective from 1 April 2019 on the basis of the Regulation on the harmonisation of the minimum gross hourly rate for the hour of temporary and temporary work. This means an increase in the minimum hourly rate of 20 % after the adoption of the novel ZUJF-C at the end of 2014. Even under the current regime, growth will be more than 10%. The proposed raising of the minimum hourly rate shall, to a certain extent, simplify the different forms of work. As the minimum student clock rises, the minimum student hours are thus equaled to the minimum wage hours. We support the proposed solutions in the Desus Group. We know that student work is a social correction of a student who needs income from the study itself. We are aware, however, that this is only part-time work and cannot be replaced by full-time employment. Nevertheless, many students and students work through a student service to improve their social status. There were 31 000 of them in 2018. For this reason, we support the proposed solutions without adding night work allowances and other planned supplements, as this would make it possible to compete with employment for a certain or indefinite period of time. Certainly we do not want to disable only student work with the rapid rise of hourly positions for student work. For this reason, we support the proposed amount of the minimum hourly item of EUR 5.4 and the amendment of the minimum wage to determine the new amount of the minimum gross hourly item by the Minister of Labour. With the annual coordination of the minimum hourly rate for student work with the growth of the minimum wage, we will come to coordination in the same way as in the temporary and occasional part of the pensioners. This will unite the occasional work of pensioners and students, which is also right. For this reason, we believe that the veto of the State Council is not justified. In the Desus Group we will once again support the proposed Law on Amendments and Supplements to the Law on Public Finance Balance. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u170	Thank you, Vice President. Dear fellows! Since the student work was introduced, without serious supervision, without taxes, contributions and working age, but as a social correction, which helps students to study in the absence of scholarship funds, a number of changes have been made in this area in recent years. The concession fee is no longer just a source of income and profits for student services and student organisations. The restrictions on fictitious entries have reduced the abuse and infamous lending of passengers. A student's job is paid for, and it's a retirement period. All of this means that the situation of the students of the workers today is much different from that of the one who did the work 10 or 15 years ago. The minimum hourly rate of student work, which is indeed set in only one Article of the ZUJF, is thus only one of the guards that has been introduced in the last decade for the benefit of student workers. The following is the point of view that each worker is equal to the worker: the law also provides that the minimum hourly rate for student work is followed by positive changes in the area of wage raising. Failure to take account of the actual situation and lack of dialogue with stakeholders have led to a point where, instead of a positive change for students, it could cause a type of inconvenience for all. Therefore, in the Group of Social Democrats, we are pleased that the debate has returned to the essence of paying students for the workers, taking into account also the significant differences in the situation of these workers compared with those of the employees, and in particular the fact that the student work has an occasional and temporary nature in its core. Exploitation of student work with excessive burdens needs to be completed, but with the intervention of inspection services, not with measures to destroy student work and break relations between workers and students. Only then will the student's work become a ticket to a real job, instead of her eternal vacation. Therefore, in the Social Democrats Group, we support the proposal that the minimum hourly rate of the student's work be calculated on the basis of the minimum wage by dividing the minimum wage by the standard monthly number of hours, as is the custom in calculating the remuneration for work. For this reason, we will also support the proposal of this law in a re-decision within the Group of Social Democrats. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2019-11-21-SDZ8-Redna-13.ana.u176	Madam President, thank you again for the floor. Dear Members, honourable Members and representative of the Council of State! Today, again before you is the amendment to the Law on Social Security Benefits, which was already approved by the National Assembly on 29 October. With a novelty, the Government abolishes the supplement to work activity and proposes to upgrade incentives for the activation of unemployed people who are recipients of monetary social assistance. The employment allowance was introduced in 2012, at the time of the crisis, when financial social assistance amounted to only EUR 230. Since cash social assistance was so low at the time, the supplement was an instrument to improve monetary social assistance. Its purpose, therefore, was to artificially increase the price for obtaining financial social assistance to the active workers. Today, the basic social assistance is 74% higher than at the time, amounting to 402 euros, and is 22 euros higher than the basic social assistance amounted to last year, together with the labour allowance. In a given situation of economic growth and the situation in the labour market where there is a shortage of labour and almost 50% of all employers are faced with a lack of adequate staff, we are facing a surge in the number of beneficiaries of financial social assistance and, on the other hand, the warnings of the Centre for Social Work and the Employment Institute that employees of individuals are no longer able to motivate them to work. This is why the Government's proposed amendment aims to motivate unemployed people to become employed and to be covered by full pension and disability insurance. This is crucial for their social security today and even in the future, when the elderly will exercise the right to a pension. At the same time, the government is proposing new incentives in the field of active employment policy and employment rehabilitation, which it considers to be more effective than the labour allowance. Long-term unemployed persons who, on the basis of the proposal before you, would be included in active employment and employment rehabilitation policies and would receive additional income for this would no longer receive a reduction in the level of financial social assistance. This arrangement is crucial in particular for those long-term unemployed people who work as volunteers every day and do not receive payment from this work, nor are they provided with pension and disability insurance. For unemployed persons, on the one hand, there will be an increase in the available income, which will not affect the level of financial social assistance and, on the other hand, will acquire knowledge and experience for a successful entry into the labour market, a step closer to the labour market. The new law obliges the Government, inter alia, to call for more jobs for long-term unemployed persons receiving financial social assistance in terms of public works. The effectiveness of these is encouraging. After six months, 32% of the participants in public works and 42% of the participants in public works receive regular employment from the employer in whom they work, or from another employer, after 12 months. The positive effects of public works are, however, particularly in the case of people with disabilities and older unemployed persons who are difficult to employ without public works in the normal labour market. Your Honor! In essence, monetary social assistance is a temporary measure aimed at survival and provides means of bridging when an individual or family is in a situation of social danger and cannot influence it. In general, the Ministry of Labour, Family, Social Affairs and Equal Opportunities for Social Transfers to Individuals and Households allocates approximately EUR 1.3 billion per annum from its budget. Many others, i.e. ministries as well as local levels, allocate additional funds for this purpose. In this way, we provide a strong security network that has the task of capturing an individual when he fails in his life, empowering him and returning him to the labour market so that he can take care of himself and his family members again. As a result, the number of beneficiaries of cash social assistance increased to 402 euros or 74 % more than it was last May, and they are also fully employed. It is with the aim of improving the situation of employees that the government has already adopted a set of measures to improve their social situation before the proposal to abolish the DDA: raising the minimum wage, decommissioning the annual leave regression, abolishing all remaining austerity measures in the area of family and social policy. The basic objective of the Government remains to ensure that each worker is able to support himself and his family members with a decent job and that all working persons, including pension and disability insurance, will receive a decent pension at their age. On the basis of the above, we propose to renew the proposal. Thank you.

You can settle all obligations under compulsory health insurance, but only when you pay that insurance, which is called supplementary, can you get really free access to a doctor, or you can only go to a doctor for payments that are not small, but can also amount to more than 100 or even more thousand euros. In short, we have agreed that this injustice will be abolished after 16 years of the promise of Slovenian politics and that this autumn will be abolished. We stuck to our part of the deal in Levica. We have prepared two laws, one which regulated the financial work, another which regulates the health work; and since then there has been an expert debate. Since the coalition did not find immediate excuses why the abolition of supplementary health insurance would not be possible, we were sent as a proposal for meetings with the Ministry, to be aligned with the profession. You probably expected us to get involved in these negotiations, but look at the fraction, the negotiations were over in one month, we agreed in more than 90%, and the professional arguments against our law were run out. Then you turned the board around and you said - no, no, it's not professional, it's political questions. And so we came in the second round this fall, when after sixteen years of waiting we got some kind of political complication, excuses why it shouldn't be possible, and finally we got that famous government response when things have to be regulated systematically. But the word systemically is a word that the Slovenian public in these good 25 years of parliamentaryism has already eaten well and means never systematically in translation. Now we're here where we are. The financial part of the law fell last week, and I am essentially saying good-bye to it before I go, but I would like to remind Members and Members once again that on 27 November we have the opportunity to adopt at least a health part of the law and to leave the finances open for one year. We've got until 1:00. 1. 2021, so more than enough. However, this is the case if we want to follow the purpose of this law, and this is to ensure fair financing of health care, then we need to follow the path that we have outlined in Levica. An alternative proposal, with which a coalition came just before the workers, after two months of arguing how things could not be terminated, and then a half-department on the committee, and not an end to the supplementary health insurance, but merely a change in its name. According to your proposal, a pensioner with 500 euros in salary and a manager with 5,000 euros in salary would continue to pay the same amount for supplementary health insurance, which would only allow them free use of health services. This places Slovenia on the world tail of countries with regard to the fairness of financing the health system, and this is also why this injustice must be removed from the system as soon as possible.

Unfortunately, we are in this state, and even after this budget, the situation will not be any better because there are no funds for these purposes and no amendments have been approved in the budgets to change this. Then we have long-term care. Rumor has it that a source should be found for her. This source is not in the budget or expenditure on building houses for the elderly, although more than 10,000 are waiting in rows for these homes. The question is what is the success of this budget. The Minister of Finance and the Prime Minister say that the greatest achievement is the surplus that we will create. In 2021, it will exceed €600 million, says more than €600 million will be on the revenue side than will be the expenditure. Now, if this is a success, you have to ask yourself where this surplus comes from. It is in the budgets, for example, next year for 600 million less money on the pension line than it was five years ago. So this surplus has come about somewhere, and it has been created mainly by tightening the belts of both the population and the development projects, and in the new decade we will have to move away from this sparpolitic that has sat down in the last decade towards development policy, which this budget does not work, so we do not have to support it.
